In the latest infamous ranking of the most polluted countries of the European Environment Agency, Poland is in second place, after Bulgaria. I would like to ask the Minister: What measures does the government intend to take to reduce the state of air pollution in Poland and thus to remove Poland from the face of this infamous ranking? ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-15-sejm-32-3.u5	I ask the Minister to answer the questions asked.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-15-sejm-32-3.u6	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Membership! Thank you kindly for this question, Mr President. I would like to answer all of your questions one at a time. So I will start by saying that there is an air protection problem across Europe, and this is not just a problem with our country, because at the moment the air protection and low emissions problems are affecting 16 European countries. At the same time, all these activities that have been undertaken by the government over the last year were, in the first place, the drafting of a regulation on boiler standards, and this is what the Ministry of Development does. Interministerial consultations have already taken place on the Regulation. The second issue related to low emissions is the Energy Minister's regulation on fuel quality and fuel standards. And this, together with the Ministry of Environment, is the Minister of Energy. Here we cooperate and cooperate with colleagues from the Ministry of Energy. And I hope that this regulation will soon be possible to publish. The problem with low emissions in Poland is that coal, which is the primary raw material for household consumption and is still the cheapest, is used by society, because it is still the cheapest fuel. Those who can afford other energy resources, of course, use them for their own use, e.g. Gas. And the wealthier citizens of our country can use this gas. Of course, gas is less emission fuel when it comes to air pollution than coal, while coal is still widely used. Therefore, we believe that the basic thing is that there should be adequate fuel on the Polish market, I am talking about carbon fuel here, and that there should be appropriate equipment for generating energy, which is here I am talking about boilers. And these two regulations should help us to fight for low emissions reduction. Another thing connected with co-financing, with the support of the National Air Protection Fund, is that today in environmental programmes, which are implemented by the National Environmental Fund, there are at least four aid lines and one dedicated specifically to the Silesian agglomeration. These lines are supposed to help fight this problem. For the first time, it was possible to use aid money for municipal heating, which in Poland we have almost 300, which have an individual network, a city heating network, to connect new customers to this network. This is a very important thing because network heat, you can say, is the cheapest heat, the cheapest energy that can be used for heating apartments or for producing hot water. With regard to the LIFE programme mentioned by the Member, the situation is that the National Fund has reviewed its programmes supporting the environment in Poland in general over the last year. It was decided that it was necessary to switch to another co-financing system for air protection in Poland. On the other hand, with regard to the LIFE programme, it was a proposal for financial assistance submitted to the beneficiary and it was possible to benefit from this proposal. With regard to the limited mobility zones of motor vehicles, this is a problem related to the emissions of pollutants from transport. Today, local governments - yesterday in the team we talked about this - have the possibility of introducing restricted transport zones and can benefit from it, although it is enough that, so to speak, local discussion and sometimes also difficult to carry out, because closed zones or limited areas of use of individual motor transport can cause some emotions. Thank you kindly.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-15-sejm-32-3.u46	Ladies and gentlemen! High Izbo! Of course, the problem of idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis is the Ministry of Health as well known for a long time. It is a disease that mainly affects patients over the age of 60. If the incidence in this group is, say, between 35 and 50 years of age, 27 cases out of 100,000, over 75 years of age, 175 patients out of 100,000. persons. The median survival time was 2.5 and 3.5 years and less than 15%. people affected by this disease are more than 10 years old. So that it is a very severe disease and two preparations available on the world and European market and registered in Poland are really just delaying the development of this disease. It is a disease, the name itself indicates - idiopathic, i.e. without a grasping cause of origin, unrelated to external factors, causing decreased activity through pulmonary fibrosis, thus reducing the gas exchange space in the lungs. His lungs are fibrous, they're less flexible, his vitals are reduced, and the patient just... he's getting more dyspnoea. You could say that the further you're choking, the more you're choking because of the reduction in gas exchange. This, of course, manifests itself with shortness of breath, reduced activity, reduced walking distance, and increased overall body failure. There are two drugs that are available, you can say, in the world. A situation that is based on the so-called. The 2011 Refund Act is as follows. The company submits an application for an assessment of the effectiveness of a given therapy to the Agency for the Evaluation of Medical Technologies and Benefits Tarification, then this assessment is subject to the appropriate position of the Transparency Board and, on the basis of these two documents, the company applies for a refund of a given medicine to the Ministry of Health. This starts with negotiations with the Economic Commission, which operates in the Ministry of Health. It is only after this entire procedural path that the Minister of Health may take and take such a refund decision. About... I'm not gonna talk about sales names, but I'm gonna talk about two preparations. The first preparation received a negative recommendation from the evaluation agency in November 2015 and a negative position from the Transparency Council on 16 November, i.e. the two main institutions negatively assessed the need for reimbursement of this preparation by the health minister. In order for the Minister of Health to make a positive decision on recommendations, he must take 13 different parameters into account. These are, in turn, the views of the Economic Commission, the recommendations of the Technology Assessment Agency, the relevance of the clinical condition concerned, the clinical efficacy of the medicine concerned, the safety of use, the health benefit-risk balance of the medicine or the method concerned, the cost-benefit ratio, the price competitiveness, the impact on the expenditure of the entity obliged to finance, i.e. the payer's budget, the existence of an alternative medical technology - whether the drug has any possible substitution by another drug, the reliability and precision of all these criteria which are assessed, the health priorities set out in the provisions of the Health Benefits Act and the threshold of the cost of an additional year of quality-adjusted life, i.e. the so-called. QALY factor, which is three times the gross domestic product. In 2016, the value of QALY, i.e. an extension of a year of age of the right quality, amounts to PLN 125 995. If the price of the drug, the annual treatment, is below this parameter, then the minister is as if he is authorized from the vending machine to make a positive decision, provided that the other elements mentioned by me are also positively assessed. If this QALY parameter is exceeded it is such a brake, a very strong financial brake to make a refund. Returning to the first medicine, this medicine received two negative recommendations from the evaluation agency and the Transparency Board in November and, despite these negative assessments, the company requested a refund. In July of this year, the Minister of Health made a negative decision, so he rejected this reimbursement application because of the lack of these most important recommendations. On the other hand, in May of this year, on 16 May, the evaluation agency issued a positive opinion, and the Transparency Council issued a positive opinion. However, it was not until October that the company, the manufacturer of this medicine, applied for a refund. The Refund Act is so constructed that the Minister of Health examines applications, but is not an active party, he cannot ask companies to submit a refund application, it is all on the side of the decision of the company, which certainly calculates many different parameters. Thus, it was not until October that the company applied for a refund, and since then talks have been taking place. There have been at least two meetings with the Economic Commission to establish [[♪ The bell ♪]] the final version, whether or not the medicine may be refunded. The case is well known and strongly publicized by the patient community, there have been many parliamentary interpellations. It is also very important that in September this year the European Parliament issued an appeal or a petition that in all European Union countries the treatment for idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis should be refunded, available.

So first of all, we're talking about really big money, and second of all, we're talking about road safety, which is based on proper winter road maintenance. Allow me to present at the moment the standard and the scheme in which winter maintenance works, which is carried out by the General Directorate of National Roads, in order to clarify this information. Well, ladies and gentlemen, the General Directorate for National Roads holds around 5%. roads in Poland. Of course, these are the most important roads: highways, express roads, national roads, except for those national roads that pass through cities on the rights of the county. And this image, which is shaped by the maintenance of roads by the general management, on which also the maintenance of roads by voivodships on voivodship roads and on district roads is imposed, it is very different. While we have clearly defined standards on national roads, which I am about to say about winter maintenance, these are three standards, but on local roads such clear, hard regulations simply do not exist. And it is also so that naturally the driver does not care whether he is driving along the way of the voivodship, district or on the national road, he simply wants a well-maintained and safe road. So that it is certain about the problem of Poland. There are situations we hear about very often in winter, for example. in radio broadcasts, that the sander drove over on a snowy road and didn't do any work, didn't pick up, didn't sprinkle. Unfortunately, this is the system of administration on Polish roads today. We have national roads, voivodship roads, county roads and every administrator is responsible for their own roads. This is the case so far, but perhaps it would be worth considering setting standards for winter maintenance, which will apply not only on national roads but also on roads administered by local authorities. But I'm sure it takes a long time to talk to local governments. I'm sure this conversation would be very long and it'd probably be hard to come up with a common position here, but I'm sure you have to try. I'm just gonna talk about the standards that apply to the general management. The first standard is approx. 4800 km of roads. It means a snowmobile, and winter slipperyness is eliminated across the whole width of the road, including hardened roadsides. There must not be a layer of snow on the road. Loose snow may fall up to 4 hours, snow mud up to 6 hours. etc. These are very clear standards. The second standard is 11 300 km of roads. It means a snowmobile, and winter slipperyness is eliminated throughout the width, including hardened roadsides. Snow loose can be up to 4 hours, snow mud up to 6 hours. There may be a layer of snow in progress with a thickness of free movement up to 6 hours. And finally the third standard is about 1800 km of roads. It means a snowmobile all over the width, and slippery is removed at intersections with paved public roads, railway lines, stretches with a slope of more than 4%, bus stops, etc. So we have very clear standards here, and we also need a general directorate as an institution responsible for maintaining simply to account and observe. If there are irregularities, it is simply necessary to speak clearly about them and intervention will be carried out. On the other hand, I would also like to say more about this. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-15-sejm-32-3.u77	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! (PL) Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, ladies and Thank you so much for that question. Questions of this type, questions about the law that you have passed, the High House has passed, asked after 4 months, I consider it a concern about how the government applies the law that Parliament has adopted in such an important area as the sealing of the tax system in a situation where the Republic needs funds to finance many projects that have not yet been implemented. That's why I'm responding. I will present a number of numbers, but I would also like to say earlier about the situation that caused the High House to proceed over this law in June and July, and we apply it. Since 2011, fuel prices have started to rise sharply. This year there was also record consumption of diesel - almost 16 million m3. In the following years, at prices still above 5 PLN, the official consumption started to decline - I stress - while the intra-Community acquisition of goods steadily increased. The conclusion is that this was the moment when criminal groups identified the enormous cost-effectiveness of the illegal business of providing cheaper, untaxed fuel. With fuel mafias, because such a term should be used, in Poland we have had a problem since the beginning of the 1990s, but after 2011 this problem was quite precisely defined. It was the result of an action in the area of intra-Community acquisitions of goods. In 2012-2014, the total consumption decreased by 14,7%. Compared to 2011 At the same time, intra-Community imports of diesel into the country reached a record value of 4.6 billion l in 2015. One might ask: What actions has the finance department taken over the years to counteract this pathology? The answer is one: no effective. However, there have also been some very psychic projects. At the turn of 2012 and 2013, the then Minister of Economy prepared a solution abolishing the requirement to have a licence for the production, storage and marketing of fuels. This was justified by the simplification of the rules of regulation in order to make it easier for new entrants to enter the market. Fortunately, this legislative initiative has not been implemented. What changes in the fuel package did he make? Change one, change in principle fuel WNT settlement. Tax collection takes place at the time of transport of fuel essentially through the payer, which makes potential fraud in the form of an unreliable monthly declaration less likely and is certainly more quickly detected by the administration. In August, 98 million PLN, 107 million PLN in September, 177 million PLN in October and 186 million PLN in November, a total of nearly 600 million PLN. Secondly, regulation of the system. There has been regulation of the system to purchase WNT fuels for another entity and excise checks. The compensation can only be paid to a Polish VAT taxable person who owns the fuel and has an OPZ licence. The need to meet all these conditions together significantly reduced the number of potential fraud actors. Currently, only 30 entities, including six entities with foreign status, fulfil these conditions, while in 2015 only foreign entities importing fuel were 65. These two, of course, are only the most important changes made to the fuel package. What was the financial impact of the fuel package? First of all, there's a sharp increase in legal sales. The data of the Polish Oil Industry and Trade Organisation shows an increase of 29 percent in August, 26 percent in September and 17 percent in October compared to last year. Despite the lower growth in fuel sales by the companies, October was the third best month of sales since 2011. Secondly, a significant decrease in intra-Community purchases of fuels to Poland through registered customers. Compared to August-October 2015 and the same period in 2016, a decrease of 63% for diesel and 51% for petrol is observed. Thirdly, we note the increase in acquisitions carried out by tax warehouses, so-called. group, as part of official imports - August-October - from 374 million l in 2015 to 569 million l in 2016. The increase in acquisitions by companies is driven by an increased demand for legal fuel. Fourthly, there is a significant reduction in the export of diesel by domestic producers. So far exports have grown from 140% to 300% each year because domestic producers were unable to sell all their production in the country. In August-October year-to-year exports with WDT decreased by 47%. Each litre of fuel not exported but sold in the country is PLN 1.17 excise duty and 80 gr VAT and 28 gr fuel levy. Mr. Marshal, thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-15-sejm-32-3.u95	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Mr. Speaker! Thank you so much for asking. And I'm responding to part one, whether all the universities have put in place a system for monitoring the fate of graduates. The entity that carries out the entire process of monitoring the fate of graduates is the Ministry. In this respect, the role of universities is limited to the proper replenishment of data in the POL-on system. And here all universities are connected to this system. These data are gradually supplemented in due course. The system itself monitors the fate of graduates works automatically and it connects two bases: POL-on base with the ZUS database, and generates reports that can be used through the graduate website.Science. gov.pl all citizens of the Republic of Poland, but this web page is dedicated especially - and here I answer the second question of the Member - to seniors or to all those who maybe a little later than immediately after graduation would like to take studies. It allows to generate a report on the employment of individual graduates, grouply, in a given direction. So it does not generate reports at the level of the entire university, although this is possible, but it allows a potential student to get answers to the question of how a particular direction at a particular university, and more specifically how the graduates from this particular university find or do not find jobs in the market. As part of the report, this high school graduate gets information about how long the graduates have been looking for work, what is the stability of their employment, or whether after they got a job, they often changed it, and the third element, he gets information about how much the graduates have been earning in this direction on average. At the same time, which is very important, this information is generated at the level of data connected also to the GUS database, i.e. they are generated at the level of powiats. If we evaluate the risk, e.g. unemployment, i.e. how long the period of looking for work is, is not given, which is given on average across the country, only this is given, which is given to the district in which this university is located and this graduate is studying, which is very important, because it allows us to see a little more precisely what the situation looks like when it comes to the fate of graduates. Sometimes we think that they find work quickly and easily only students in major universities, in large cities. It may be a surprise to some that sometimes smaller universities in smaller towns prepare their graduates much better for the needs of the local labour market. Thus, since the announcement of the first reports, which were published on 18 May 2016, the Ministry is now taking action to promote this instrument, because its effectiveness will depend on how many potential candidates for studies simply generate the right for themselves, for them individually... That's why it's valuable in this system that he very individualizes this information for a student. By the way, if I may say, this is one of the first systems in Europe to monitor the fate of graduates in this respect. Only the Swedes have the same, but our system is much more advanced, it processes much more data, so that it is some kind of social innovation, if you can say so, in the field of monitoring the fate of graduates. And what's very important is that we work on data, not opinions. These are not surveys, these are real, hard data that are connected to the system, as I said, POL-on and the ZUS system. Thank you very much.

Pathologists in the labour market, the fight against garbage contracts. The result of one such decision, which we have taken, is the entry into force of 1 September of the amendments to the Labour Code concerning the elimination of the so-called. First-day syndrome. Let me remind you that until now the employment contract could have been received by the end of the first day of work. Now, after this change, the employment contract has to be delivered before admission to work. This should to a large extent minimise circumvention of the law. The State Labour Inspection came for inspection, the employer claimed it was work on the first day, and it was known that this work had already been done for several months. It is worth noting here that for the first time in the history of the minimum wage negotiations there has been a situation that the trade union side has proposed a lower amount in terms of the increase in the minimum wage. The government decided that it would amount to PLN 2,000. This also involves a very important amendment that we have made, i.e. the introduction of the 12th and from 1 January the 13th hour rate at the contract order. This should significantly eliminate pathologies that have been on the Polish labour market so far. We had contracts for taro-gold contracts, and even a couple hundred and ten-dollar contracts - I saw a contract for 25 gr per hour. The introduction of this into contract contracts and where there are self-employed persons should make such pathology impossible. To do this, an amendment to the law has to be added, which has resulted in the contract of the order being reduced from 1 January to the minimum wage. That too, I think, will cause that pathology to go away. In addition, we have strengthened the State Labour Inspection, which at the moment will be able to control the use of such contracts illegally, it is about escape or it is about works contracts, whether in a different form, among others. self-employment. If this is the way it comes to the debate, it is difficult to discuss with the people who come here - I am talking about Members, not all Members, from the opposition. 'Cause if we're giving low unemployment figures, we're bragging. So, what are we supposed to do if we give up-to-date data on unemployment? We're supposed to say it's bad, what's the lowest unemployment rate in 25 years? Some kind of schizophrenia complete. But we already need to get used to the fact that, in any situation, even with regard to the labour market, we need to bring this element of political struggle. And it is, you can say, a post-political one, because the labour market issue, workers' rights affect all of us. And that's the first general remark. I've divided these questions into a few groups of questions. I will also try to answer specific questions. First of all, on issues related to support for young people. Here, too, statistics are definitely improving. We've got 15,6% right now. people up to the age of 24 who are in unemployment statistics. There is more than 18% in the European Union. This trend is decreasing. Employment rates and employment rates are also increasing there. This shows that the direction we have chosen, i.e. support for young people, whether it is a reduction of up to 4 months of time when the employment office is obliged to present an offer or an exemption from the employer's minimum wage in the first year of work for young people up to 30 years of age, or a training voucher or an internship voucher - all this makes the situation of young people improve. This does not mean that there is no problem that, when it comes to these people, we do not still need to direct this policy to young people. One of these issues that we are talking about is also the issue of wages and minimum wages. There have been numerous mention of the minimum wage. Well, if we want to talk about a decent reward, it's a minimum if we're talking about a minimum wage, but also in the context of stopping young people who are leaving our country. If there is a big pay gap, this situation is detrimental to us. Today, most of you have spoken of this here. We are dealing with a situation where there is a high demand for the labour market for workers. This must also be due to the fact that the increase in wages is also greater - 2 000. zł. There's no danger today that this issue is excessive. In our strategy, which we have adopted, which the social side is also looking for, I am talking about trade unions, we are going to have a minimum wage of 50%. This is also due to EU law. We're at 47%. This is a good time. I will respond to Mr Husband on the criteria for income. Income criteria are changing on 1 November. At the moment, there is not yet a government decision whether this criterion will be increased, but I will remind you that recently this criterion has already been increased. This case is before the decision, which is why there is no answer. But you have asked for this in your interpretions, sir, so this answer will be provided. There has often been a thread on issues related to the implementation of the 500+ programme. If you already have all the data indicating that 500+ is deactivating women after a few months of implementation of the programme, then you are mistaken. No data confirms this - in fact, the opposite. The statistics I presented show that the labour market for women is continuing, [[Applause]] and one of the messengers, I think Mrs Masłowska spoke about it. She said that even if a woman who has several children stays at home with these children, she won't work for the lowest salary, it would be a better solution for this family than what is today. That's why there's no fear. The program's working. The program's fine. When it comes to complaining to some employers that they have to raise their salaries because women want to quit, let them raise their salaries. That's the right thing to do. No one in any data absolutely confirms this situation. The question of disabled people. The labour market situation of people with disabilities is difficult. She's getting better, but at an unsatisfactory rate, so to speak. Other changes still need to be made here. Here, too. We are making changes regarding the conditions for the settlement of social security contributions by disabled people, the number of establishments is increasing. This is the answer to Mrs Krynicka's question. Currently, over 4,000 employees work in 100 professional activation plants. persons with disabilities. In this direction, these changes, which will be undertaken both by the PFRON board and by the Ministry, are also continued. The Member asked about the matter of the State Labour Inspection. The State Labour Inspection is subject to the Sejm, not to the Ministry, but I can assure you - in my experience - that there is no situation so that labour inspectors especially want to close the plant. I don't know any such situation. There's got to be some kind of security situation. That is probably why, according to the regulations, the inspector made a recommendation to correct this situation. If this is a specific situation, please ask the Chief Labour Inspector to clarify the matter. But in my experience, that's not the situation. In general, when it comes to inspection of work, it was very pro-working. We want to change this trend, reverse it, so that workers' rights are more protected. In a fight the employer - the employee is always in a worse situation. On the other hand, it has been said many times that the State Labour Inspection has great possibilities of punishment. We have the lowest fines in the European Union as regards fines. In France, for example, the simplest offence is punished with a minimum of 2 000. Euros. These are the amounts that inspectors give there. But this PIP thing, like I said, it's not our thing. But in my experience, this case is clean. There have been many questions about issues related to temporary work. Indeed, as far as the agency for temporary employment is concerned, there is a big problem because in recent years it has been assumed that most of the temporary employment agencies have been employed. The agency itself and the temporary work itself is nothing wrong, but if it is abused, that is, the same person is constantly employed by the temporary employment agency, which means there is no permanent employment, that is pathology. We want to change that. The so-called permanent work. a round table with social partners to make changes. Among other things, we want to end the phenomenon that we are dealing with, the creation of fictitious temporary employment agencies that employ workers, do not pay wages, do not pay contributions. Therefore, the agency will be controlled by the marshal offices under which it is subject, whether the ZUS-owska contribution is deducted from the salaries of such employees. But we also want to make a change in the amendment of this law so that the person employed has a job guarantee, but it is also not used by other agencies and employed by the same employer for longer periods. This issue will be settled at least in the area where the agreement between the partners and the social partners has taken place. As regards issues related to Silesia - a very important subject, thank you very much, Mr President, for once again correcting the false information that Mr Gadowski gives, because it is simply not true - it is not that we are eliminating and people remain unemployed. For miners there are jobs in other companies. There is a question of mining leave - it also somehow solves this problem - and also through the voivodship labour office we have launched consultative points, which among others. they help those companies that operate around the mining industry, because that's where the biggest problem is. They're not included in these packages. They can be helped with instruments that we have already mentioned here today. It's about the labour fund. We've got the resources. If necessary, we'll run the labour fund reserve. But here the county labour offices and the provincial labour office are more capable of acting, because from there we need to know what we can run here. But it's continued here. We are making changes to the business environment laws that make it easier for entrepreneurs to function. This direction is also intended to help entrepreneurs to create jobs, but also to employ workers. The issue of employment irregularities. There was also a question about the minimum hourly rate. Of course, this minimum hourly rate applies to all entities, including local authorities, and institutions, and does not apply to natural persons who agree to perform activities for other institutions. Yes, we want to reduce those pathologies that concerned very low wages again, and I think that this amount of 13 PLN will definitely improve. The questions also concerned actions for the elderly. This is the second group, except for the young people who are most at risk. The indicators are also improving considerably here, but we also have programmes under the Act that concern older people. Only in 10 months, 70,000. people over the age of 50 have activated, i.e. 16% all activated persons. This is more than last year, but of course we still need to make different changes here so that we also have job offers for these people. I think I answered most of your questions here. As I said, I have divided these questions into different blocks to answer completely the issues that you have raised. And as I said at the beginning, it's hard to say here that if we have a low unemployment rate, we're gonna be sad. Unemployment is low, that is good news, but we are aware of the problems in the labour market, that unemployment is diverse, that we have a problem with long-term unemployed, on which we must devote far greater resources to their activation. We also need to make a change, because there was also quite a lot of pathology in this case, which shouldn't have happened. As far as the amendment of the Act on the Promotion of Labour Market Institutions is concerned, we are preparing, gathering information, we have a review of the 2-year law that has been amended. We also had, personally, several dozen meetings with representatives of labour offices, with entrepreneurs. We want to prepare new, comprehensive solutions, but we need time for that too. The information I've been collecting will allow us to work out the best possible solutions. In general, we want to simplify this law more, make it more flexible so that, at the level of labour offices, it is easier to use labour market instruments both for entrepreneurs and for those looking for jobs. High Izbo, the Extraordinary Commission on Changes in Codifications, has examined at its last meeting on the draft law of the Code of Civil Procedure. The purpose of this proposal was to implement the judgment of the Constitutional Court, which ruled that Article. 408 The Code of Civil Procedure is incompatible with the Constitution to the extent that, 5 years after the judgment was passed, it does not allow for the reopening of proceedings on grounds of nullity resulting from the infringement of the Article. 6 the European Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms. Therefore, the committee leaned on the submission of the government, which in fact extends the deadline described in the Article. 408 to 10 years old, which, according to the government, will allow full implementation of all those claims that have so far been potentially possible with such a 5-year time frame. The Commission has welcomed this government proposal by introducing two legislative and editorial amendments in the article. 408 and Article 408 2 of this proposal for a law. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-15-sejm-32-3.u219	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I present my position on a proposal to amend the provisions of the Civil Procedure Code, specifically Article. 408. This government submission was the result of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal delivered by the rapporteur, so-called. the judgment of 22 September 2015 stating that Article. 408 to the extent that, 5 years after the judgment was passed, it does not allow for the reopening of the proceedings on grounds of nullity resulting from the infringement of the Article. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 The Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms is incompatible with Article. Article 77 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 in conjunction with Article. Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Constitution of the Republic of Poland. I'll remind you, Art. Article 77 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 provides that the law must not close anybody's judicial path to the investigation of violated rights and freedoms and Article. Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 concerns the right to court. Everyone shall have the right to a judicial decision within a reasonable period of time by a competent, impartial, independent and independent court. The proceedings before the European Court of Human Rights are usually slightly longer than this 5-year period, which is also a direct prerequisite for this amendment. I would like to add here that the Constitutional Tribunal has deferred the expiry of the existing Article. 408 c.p.c. for 18 months as from 3 April 2017 It is also worth recalling that already in the case-law of the courts, especially in the votes to those judgments, as well as in the doctrine of civil proceedings, there are such requests that this 5-year period for the reopening of proceedings should be extended. Let me remind you that the term in the article. 408 has been in force since 1 January 1965. This amendment complies with these requirements, except that it has been extended to all grounds for renewal, which are not only based on the judgments of the European Court of Human Rights, as well as on other grounds for renewal mentioned in the article. 408 Code of Civil Procedure. A transitional provision is also introduced to provide that in cases initiated before the date of entry into force of this Act in which that 5-year period has not expired, a 10-year period as amended by the law shall apply. As far as vacathio legis is concerned, it is a basic minimum, 14 days from the date of publication of the law. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice calls for the support of this government proposal. Does the proposed amendment address all these matters to the end? I'm sure time will show, but on behalf of the Civic Platform Club, I wanted to tell you that the Platform Club will support this chosen form of art amendment. 408 Civil Code and the manner in which the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal is enforced. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-15-sejm-32-3.u222	Thank you. And Paul Grabowski, please. There isn't. This is Marshal Barbara Dolniak, please.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-15-sejm-32-3.u223	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! It follows from the justification of the draft law that the amendment of the article. 408 c.p.c. is related to the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal SK 21/14. The Court found this provision incompatible with the Constitution, but only to the extent that, 5 years after the judgment was passed, it does not allow for the reopening of proceedings on grounds of nullity resulting from the infringement of the Article. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 the Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms. The Court pointed out the need to introduce a separate one from that laid down in Article. 408 c.p.c. the time limit for lodging an action for reopening civil proceedings on grounds of nullity of that procedure, the reason for which was the infringement of the Article. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 of the Conventions established by the final judgment of the ECHR. Therefore, the constitutional reservations did not relate to the length of the pre-litigation term itself, but to the fact that it could disappear before the party to whom the complaint was taken into account by the ECHR could exercise its rights under national law or the exercise of those rights would be hindered. The elimination of this threat may take the form not only of an extension of the deadline - and there is considerable doubt here as long as, after all, we cannot predict each case how long the proceedings before the ECHR will last - but also of the withdrawal of that period in a situation where the party refers to the fact that the basis for the renewal was learned from the ECHR judgment. However, the proposed law provides for an extension of the five-year time limit for bringing an action for the reopening of proceedings in all cases where the renewal is permitted by the provisions of the c.p.c. without limiting the scope of the application of the new time limit to the case of a violation of the Constitution. The explanatory memorandum also states that the introduction of a separate pre-emptive period for the renewal of proceedings only for cases where the grounds for renewal are based on an infringement of the ECHR Convention would be an asystemic solution for the current regulation of the reopening of proceedings, as the provisions on this issue do not distinguish the resumption of proceedings due to the ECHR judgment. This view cannot be accepted if it is considered that the catalogue of grounds for reopening civil proceedings has been extended several times, examples: Article. 408, Art. 403 § 4 or Art. 416 c.p.c. In the statement of reasons set out above, the Constitutional Tribunal expressly stated that it did not call into question the usefulness, necessity of a five-year period for bringing an action for the renewal of civil proceedings. It is therefore appropriate to amend the Article. 408 c.p.c. adding to the exceptions set out in that provision such a solution where the party indicates that the basis for the renewal was learned from the ECHR judgment. The extension of the pre-emptive period to lodge a complaint for the renewal from 5 to 10 years will not definitively eliminate the possibility of an actual situation, which was the basis for the finding of the unconstitutionality of the Article by the Constitutional Tribunal. 408 c.p.c. The modification concerns only the abolition of a 5-year limitation in the event of a reopening action for a party which looks back on the deficiencies identified by the ECHR and which the party qualifies as the basis for renewal. The absence of a limitation of this 5-year deadline does not constitute a novel, as it already occurs in the cases referred to in Article. 408 c.p.c., while leaving this restriction to the rest of the grounds for renewal will serve to preserve the stability and sustainability of final judgments constituting one of the elements of the rule of law and thus also the values protected by the Convention. The duration of proceedings before the ECHR continues to extend and it cannot be excluded that only shortly before the expiry of 10 years after the end of the judgment in the case, the party will obtain a judgment of the ECHR declaring certain actions of the State which would fall within the grounds for the resumption set out in the c.p.c. as violating the Convention and the party will not have full 3 months to bring an action for renewal. The fact that the proceedings can be reopened for so long may make it impossible to effectively bring an action for the finding of unlawfulness and, consequently, deprive the parties of the possibility of effectively seeking compensation for a judicak delict. It should also be pointed out that, contrary to the statements in the statement of reasons in the literature, the subject matter of the amendment of the article. 408 c.p.c. by the extension of the period indicated therein shall not be notified. The draft provides for an inter-temporal provision, the provision of which raises a constitutional objection. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Therefore, the modern amendment to this draft law is tabled. The provisions of this Act extend the time limit within which an action for the reopening of legal proceedings can be brought from 5 to 10 years. Although the main objective of the amended amendment is the execution of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 22 September 2015, it is not limited only to cases which were the basis of that judgment. Regardless of the grounds for filing a complaint, citizens will have 10 years to do so under the new rules. As has been repeated many times, extending this deadline will allow citizens to exercise their rights more freely. Despite the reservations which the Supreme Court and the National Court of Justice sent to the project, the reservations that the new deadlines should apply only to the addressees of the judgments of the European Court of Human Rights, I agree with the arguments of the projectors. The distinction of new conditions for renewal would entail additional complications and difficulties. The solution proposed is therefore, in our view, an appropriate response to the judgment of the Constitutional Court. About Free and Solidarni supports the government bill. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-15-sejm-32-3.u235	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present to the High House at first reading a draft law on auditors, audit firms and public oversight, print No. 1092. The draft law aims at adapting to the Polish law solutions provided for by European Union law, introducing a reform of EU rules on audit of financial statements. That's what I call it. the EU audit package. It concerns the implementation of Directive 2014/56/EU amending Directive 2006/43/EC and Regulation No 537/2014 on detailed requirements for statutory audit of public-interest entities. The new rules introduce a wide range of changes aimed at strengthening the independence and objectivity of statutory auditors and audit firms by significantly reducing and eliminating the conflict of interest between statutory auditors and audit firms and the audited entities, in particular the audited JZP. The aim is to improve the quality of statutory audits by improving the quality of audit reporting to different addressees, entities, regular dialogue between audit firms, as well as supervisory authorities, strengthening the role and tasks of the audit committee in the audit firm's election process. It is also intended to strengthen public oversight by strengthening its independence from the environment of auditors. The bill presented includes these solutions in the national legal order, introduces modifications of the current system solutions in the area of research and public oversight of audit in order to comply with the new requirements of the Union. It retains certain provisions already in force which do not require amendment, completes the transpositions of the existing provisions of Directive 2006/43/EC in order to improve the current regulations and facilitate their application and interpretation, and complements the implementation of Directive 2013/34/EU on annual accounts, consolidated accounts and related reports of certain types of entities. The most important arrangements for transposing the Directive and enabling the Regulation to be applied are a new model of public oversight and oversight tasks, the designation of the audit committee of the KNA as a public oversight body for statutory auditors and audit firms. The KNA will now have the power to carry out planned, ad hoc and thematic audits in audit firms investigating JZP, investigate and prosecute as prosecutors before courts on disciplinary offences committed in connection with statutory audit of public trust units, conduct administrative proceedings for the imposition of penalties on audit firms in relation to irregularities arising in the examination of a public interest unit. The penalties and sanctions for statutory auditors on disciplinary offences committed in connection with the JPA statutory audit will be imposed by common courts. It is also about monitoring the concentration in the research market, financial statements, the competitiveness of audit firms, the supervision of tasks entrusted to the National Chamber of Auditors, the appointment of the Financial Supervision Commission, the KNF as a public oversight body for public trust units. In accordance with the new regulations, KNF will supervise the JZP's selection of the audit firm and its rotation and rotation of the key auditor, impose penalties and sanctions on JZP, members of the supervisory board, audit committee and associated persons, and monitor the activities of audit committees in public trust units. There will also be a delegation of certain tasks of public oversight to be carried out by professional authorities, assuming that the Audit Supervisory Board is ultimately responsible for overseeing these tasks. The KIBR body, the National Supervisory Commission, the KKN will have the power to carry out scheduled and ad hoc audits of auditors and audit firms in the field of statutory audits of entities other than JZP, to carry out audits of auditors and audit firms in the field of services covered by national standards for the pursuit of the profession other than statutory audit. There will be a narrowing of the scope of public oversight and public oversight only to statutory audits. The new catalogue of public interest units of JZP will be imported, i.e. it will be reduced to 1932 units compared to the current 1994. It will include entities whose securities are listed on a regulated market and which are subject to statutory audit obligations, credit institutions, national banks, branches of credit institutions and branches of foreign banks and insurance undertakings, open pension funds, general pension societies, open investment funds, specialised investment funds, public investment funds closed, large SKOKs and certain brokerage entities, large electronic money institutions. SKOK-i, electronic money institutions that do not meet the definition of a large unit are excluded from the JZP directory. This includes regulations and restrictions on the provision of services, research and services other than research for the benefit of the audited client. Mandatory regulation of the audit firm investigating JZP - maximum every 5 years, 4 years of withdrawal. The minimum period of employment of an audit firm is 2 years, as proposed in the project. Mandatory rotation of the key auditor for JZP surveys - maximum every 5 years plus 3 years of withdrawal. Gradual rotation of the most important subjects in the study teams. The black list of prohibited services, such as in the Regulation, has not been extended. This list of prohibited services includes services such as participation in the management and decision-making process of JZP, accounting and financial statements, development and implementation of internal control procedures or risk management procedures, admission to provide the audited public trust and tax advisory and valuation services, 70% limit of remuneration for non-black services provided to the audited JZP, 50% limit of remuneration for services subject to conditional exemption from the provision ban, tax advice and valuation. Strengthening the process and rules for selecting audit firms are the next provisions of this law. The obligation to have an audit committee, a KA, by all public trust units and the ability of the supervisory board or other supervisory body to act as KA has been introduced. Other provisions set out new requirements for the organisation of internal audit firms and the reporting of the audit carried out, while the new catalogue of penalties and persons subject to penalty for infringement of the provisions of the Act and the Regulation will appear to be punishable by statutory auditors and audit firms, bodies of public interest, entities associated with JZP, third parties related to JZP, and subcontractors of audit firms or other persons and entities who participated in the activities, studies or could influence the outcome of the audit. The maximum amount of the fine is 250,000. PLN in the case of natural persons and 10% in the case of natural persons income in the case of legal persons. The Act also lays down new rules on the financing of the public oversight system. The supervisory fee for the quality of JZP statutory audits will be a maximum of 5,5% the revenue of audit firms for the execution of financial audit activities and will be paid directly by audit firms to the State budget. In 2017, this fee will not be charged at the maximum rate, its rate is intended in this Act, set at 5,15%. The fee for supervision of the quality of statutory audits of non-JZP, i.e. other entities, will be up to 1,5% the revenue of audit firms for the execution of financial audit activities in non-JZP and will be paid by audit firms directly to the account of the National Chamber of Auditors. The percentage of this fee will be in 2017, as assumed, 1,34%. Entry into force of the Act. The draft law will in principle enter into force 14 days after its publication, with the exception of the provisions on supervisory fees which enter into force on 1 January 2017. The provisions of Regulation No 537/2014 on detailed requirements for statutory audit of public-interest entities should be applied directly by the entities to which it is addressed from 17 June 2016. Thank you kindly.

Thus, the Government's bill on auditors, audit firms and public oversight of print No. 1092 is intended in particular to: increase the independence of auditors and thereby improve the quality of audit of financial statements, strengthen public oversight measures over the activities of audit firms and auditors, regulate the provision by auditors of services other than audit of financial statements, regulate the mandatory rotation of audit firms and key auditors, define a catalogue of public interest entities to which stricter statutory requirements are applied, and create real sanctions for infringement of the law or regulation. And the employers continue to write: Poland should implement a total ban on statutory auditors to provide services other than directly related to the financial audit. What do we think should be pointed out in the course of further parliamentary work? Firstly, to prohibit the provision of services other than strictly related to auditing financial statements. We believe that the auditor has an extremely important task of examining the financial condition of external companies - so he cannot participate in its formation, cannot advise business and tax, because then he evaluates the effects of his own advice and becomes an involved entity. Secondly, we want to draw attention to the definition of the network. In order to be effective, the creation of structures by audit firms should be prevented in order to circumvent the rules, e.g. the division of audit companies or the appointment of subsidiaries in order to provide both audit services and broadly understood advisory services. Thirdly, the definition of an individual of public interest so as to ensure transparency and market security as far as possible by recognising as JZP [[♪ The bell ♪]] all economic players. Finally, fourthly, the obligation to rotate so as to improve the quality of the study by reducing the audit's dependence on the audited entity, so that this dependence is as small as possible to make this rotation real. The project is a government project worth working on to improve the stability and development of the Polish market. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-15-sejm-32-3.u239	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club on the government bill on auditors, audit firms and public oversight, print No. 1092. The aim of the proposed law is to implement into the Polish legal order the new provisions of European Union audit law, including Directive 2014/56 of the European Union on statutory audits of annual accounts and consolidated accounts and Regulation 537/2014 on detailed requirements for statutory audits of financial statements of public-interest entities. The Directive contains the basic provisions for all audit firms and auditors, while the Regulation includes additional requirements for audit firms and statutory auditors investigating public-interest entities. The new Community legislation introduces a number of changes aimed, first, at strengthening the independence and objectivity of statutory auditors and audit firms by significantly reducing and eliminating conflicts of interest between statutory auditors and audit firms and audited entities, in particular audited public-interest entities, second, improving the quality of statutory audits by improving the quality of audit reporting to different addressees, regular dialogue between audit firms and supervisory authorities of credit institutions and insurance undertakings, additional control of the quality of the execution of the audit order, in particular public-interest entities, thirdly strengthening public oversight by strengthening its independence from the environment of auditors and enhancing supervisory powers. The new rules of the European Union are part of the regulatory reform in various areas of the financial sector and are the result of action taken by the European Commission in response to the global crisis in 2007. The aim is to restore confidence in markets and financial institutions and strengthen the stability of the financial system. The new rules aim to prevent errors and irregularities in auditors' reports, lack of reliability and reliability of audited financial statements, excessive intimacy between the management of the audited entity and the audit firm, threats to the independence of the statutory auditor and others. Unfortunately, the draft law in many places raises very serious concerns, which require a fundamental change. Thus, the proposed definition of a public-interest entity significantly narrows down the current catalogue of these entities, particularly in the area of cooperative savings and credit banks. Currently, all SKOKs are included in the catalogue of public-interest entities, while the introduction of this definition will result in only two cash registers and one cash in bankruptcy for SKOKs. Banks as deposit-taking entities whose payment is covered by the guarantee scheme should all be treated as public-interest entities as public funds cover the costs of their bankruptcy. And they've been avalanche lately. The exclusion of most SKOKs from this definition limits part of the supervisory powers, which in particular in this case is absolutely unjustified, and even, I will say more, dangerous. The Act of Procedure will replace the Act of May 2009 currently in force. Much of the legislation comes from that law. As a result of the public consultation, the comments submitted to the draft law were partially taken into account, but some of them remained not covered by the Ministry. They are very important, and therefore, in the process of further legislation, they require a specific, in-depth analysis. An example of this is the Commission's comment on the catalogue of entities defined as public-interest entities that I was talking about. The adoption of this definition will mean reducing public oversight to a minimum and could jeopardise the achievement of the important objective of EU regulation, which is to achieve a high level of protection for consumers and investors in capital markets. This threat must be eliminated in further work. Notwithstanding the above comments and reservations, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club recommends a bill for further work in the Public Finance Commission. Thank you for your attention. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-15-sejm-32-3.u257	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I present a government bill on the exchange of tax information with other countries. The Act is crucial to fulfilling the obligations arising from a number of directives which require Poland to provide information on certain tax and property aspects relating to nationals of other countries on a reciprocal basis. We are entitled to expect symmetrical information about the assets of our citizens in bank accounts in other countries, which will lead to the sealing of the tax systems of the European Union and the elimination of certain abuses and undesirable practices in international trade or in the establishment of international structures. As I said, the Act provides for the exchange of information on financial accounts held by non-residents, individual tax rulings, transaction pricing agreements, protective opinions on the tax avoidance clause and so-called. country-by-country reporting, i.e. groups of entities, capital groups, their composition and size of business, e.g. about revenue achieved, broken down by jurisdiction. The central part of the Act is also the implementation of the Common Reporting Standard system obliging Polish financial institutions to identify, collect and transmit information on their accounts to non-residents to the tax administration. On the basis of reciprocity Poland will receive, as I said, similar information about financial accounts held by Polish residents in foreign institutions. We do not foresee significant costs of implementing this law, but we expect that this information exchange system will result in some sealing both within personal income taxes and corporate income tax. In connection with the time limits imposed by the directive, we are counting on the swift adoption of the law. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-15-sejm-32-3.u258	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Jerzy Gosievski, Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-15-sejm-32-3.u259	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Dear Minister, On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the Government's draft Tax Information Exchange Act with other countries, print No. 1091. The proposed law sets out the rules, modalities and characteristics of the authorities for the exchange of tax information with other countries. It also lays down the obligations of a financial institution for the exchange of tax information and for the automatic exchange of tax information on Reportable Accounts and for the control of their execution, introduces a regulation enabling the automatic exchange of information in the field of taxation with non-EU countries on the basis of the CRS - Common Reporting Standard procedures. This bill implements several Council directives into the Polish legal order and amends six laws that concern tax obligations. The need for mutual assistance between different tax administrations in the exchange of broadly understood tax information is becoming increasingly important. Due to the huge increase in taxpayers' mobility, the number of cross-border transactions and the internationalisation of financial instruments, it is becoming increasingly difficult to determine the level of the bases due. Without mutual cooperation between tax administrations, a single Member State cannot effectively combat tax fraud and tax evasion. In order to overcome these bad effects, it is essential to create an appropriate system of cooperation between tax administrations. In recent years, the challenges of tax fraud and cross-border tax evasion have come significantly. Undisclosed and untaxed income reduces national tax revenues. Action is therefore needed to improve the effectiveness of the fight against tax fraud and tax evasion by strongly promoting the automatic exchange of information as a future European international standard for transparency and exchange of information in tax matters. The importance of automatic exchange of information as one of the measures against tax fraud and cross-border tax evasion has been recognised at international level by the G20 and the G8. Effective administrative cooperation between Member States is needed under conditions consistent with the proper functioning of the internal market by strengthening tools, information exchange mechanisms. The proposed amendments introduce transparency requirements for entities forming part of a group of entities. Greater transparency should encourage these groups of actors to reduce inappropriate practices. It should also be pointed out that, since 2013, extensive efforts have been carried out to reduce tax fraud and tax evasion within both the European Union and the OECD. The proposed law is another element in the whole package of good change, which will increase the revenue of the state in billions of zlotys. The legal solutions adopted in the proposed law will significantly increase the effectiveness of the exchange of tax information. The scope of reporting procedures depends on whether the account is a high-value account. It is appropriate, in fact, to significantly reduce tax fraud, particularly international taxpayers, with huge amounts of money. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice fully supports the draft law on the exchange of tax information with other countries in print No. 1091. Thank you very much, Marshal.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-15-sejm-32-3.u261	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour to present the position of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club on the government draft law on the exchange of tax information with other countries, print no. 1091. The purpose of the draft law is to regulate in one legal act issues relating to the exchange of tax information, as well as the very important objective of implementing the directives in the Polish legal order: Council Directive 2014/107/EU of December 2014 amending the Directive on mandatory automatic exchange of information in the field of taxation, Directive 2015 of 10 November 2015, Council Directive 2015/2376 of December 2015 and Council Directive 2016/881 of 25 May 2016. It is also important to introduce regulations enabling the automatic exchange of information in the field of taxation with non-EU countries based on existing procedures to comply with the obligation of the Republic of Poland in the multilateral agreement on the automatic exchange of financial information signed in October 2014. The proposed law standardizes the rules and procedures for the exchange of tax information with other countries, the obligations of financial institutions in the field of automatic exchange of tax information and the control of their implementation. The need for these measures is due to the huge increase in taxpayers' mobility and associated cross-border transactions, which seriously makes it difficult for tax authorities to properly measure their taxes. The problems of tax authorities in obtaining the necessary tax information are often used by taxpayers to tax evasion and even tax fraud. The lack of close cooperation between tax administrations in individual countries makes it ineffective and, moreover, every year these phenomena are becoming more and more widespread. The creation of a coherent system of cooperation between tax authorities in individual countries is therefore not only necessary but very urgent. For several years, the European Union has been making a cyclical diagnosis of the level of these adverse events in individual countries. On this basis, it considered it urgent to put in place mechanisms to prevent tax avoidance and fraud and thus increase the efficiency and efficiency of tax collection. This objective is served by automatic exchange of information as a European and international standard for transparency and exchange of information in tax matters. This is defined by Directive 2014/107/EU, whose implementation into the Polish legal order is the fundamental subject of the contested law. It is appropriate to agree that such significant and extensive legislation, in particular the implementation of EU law, is in favour of regulating the exchange of tax information in a single legal act. The draft law was subject to a public consultation on a shorter time limit for the submission of an opinion. According to the report submitted, only part of the comments were taken into account. The draft law requires urgent procedure, since the transposition deadline has already expired or will expire soon, only the 2016 Directive has a longer deadline - until 4 June 2017. In the overall assessment of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform requires and is important for the tax system and facilitates the activities of the tax administration. However, it requires a detailed and calm analysis in the Committee on Public Finance and an in-depth analysis of the comments submitted during the consultation. There can be no rush here, because hurry always carries the risk of making mistakes. Therefore, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club recommends that the bill be directed to further legislative work. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-15-sejm-32-3.u263	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, I have the honour to present my position on the governmental draft law on the exchange of tax information with other countries, together with draft implementing acts, that is, print no. 1091. The main objective of this project is to implement EU law introducing regulations related to the exchange of tax information between countries. When analysing transactions and financial flows at a time of globalisation, which we have, it must be said that the number of cross-border transactions is increasing, the activity of taxpayers is increasing and it is becoming increasingly difficult to properly determine the amount of taxes due. This situation is obviously conducive to tax evasion at precisely this cross-border level, as well as to the emergence of international tax fraud. In order to increase the efficiency and efficiency of tax collection, it was necessary to establish administrative cooperation systems between tax administrations of different countries. These expectations are met by the European Commission's directives. The main subject matter of this proposed Tax Information Exchange Act with other countries is introduced by Directive 2014/107/EU of 9 December 2014 as regards the mandatory automatic exchange of information in the field of taxation. The project also implements Council Directive 2015/2060 of 10 November 2015 on taxation of savings income in the form of interest payments as well as two Directives, i.e. Directive 2015/2376 of 8 December 2015 and Directive 2016/881 of 25 May 2016 as regards mandatory automatic exchange of information in the field of taxation. The draft law takes into account OECD elements of the global standard, known as the CRS, for the automatic exchange of financial information in tax matters and also applies to countries outside the Union. The whole CRS package was approved by the G20 finance ministers and central bank governors in September 2014 and Poland signed an appropriate multilateral agreement on automatic exchange of financial information in October 2014. This basic Directive 2014/107, as well as the draft law, are based on this CRS procedure and establish uniform reporting rules, due diligence rules in all Member States, as well as in other countries outside the Union. The bill contains a very comprehensive vocabulary of concepts and definitions, protecting against divergent interpretation of the provisions of the Act. Very detailed, with great care, lays down rules for the exchange of information, at the request of the Office, with the Member States, of automatic exchange of information on reported accounts, defines the obligations of reporting financial institutions, lays down rules for the implementation of reporting procedures. The project also regulates the rules for the exchange of information on tax interpretations and decisions on transaction prices in entities forming part of a group of entities. The project was subject to a large number of public consultations. Some of the comments submitted were taken into account and others remained to be re-examined. The draft also contains rules on penalties. Here, one negative assessment is that the duality of this sanctioning is introduced, i.e. through administrative and criminal penalties. These agreements shall normally be ratified with the prior consent of the law and on the basis of Article. 87 and Article 87. 91 of the Constitution becomes part of Polish law. They may be applied directly and, in the event of a conflict with the law, they shall have priority over their application. In view of the relations between Poland and China, it is necessary to exclude the conclusion of an agreement between the governments or the finance ministers of the parties. The concept, which was adopted, and this is also a proposal from the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, is a concept of a law transposing an agreement between commercial offices into Polish law, approving the agreement and ensuring its execution in Polish law, subject to reciprocity. This agreement is part of the draft law. This reciprocity between Taipei and Warsaw is preserved here, and therefore the Act pursues certain objectives from the point of view of taxpayers. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-15-sejm-32-3.u272	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Dear Minister, On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the 1162 report of the Committee on Public Finance on the Government's draft law on the avoidance of double taxation and the prevention of tax evasion on income taxes between Poland and the territory to which tax law belonging to the jurisdiction of the Minister of Finance of Taiwan applies, print No 1151. During the work of the Committee on Public Finance, as presented by the rapporteur of the committee, Mr Soboń, a number of changes have been made, legislative changes to the original draft, including the title of the law. The proposed solutions in the government bill form a legal framework on the basis of which the principles of mutual cooperation in the field of income taxation will be laid down by transposing the provision concluded on 21 October 2016 between the Warsaw Trade Office in Taipei and the Taipei Economic and Cultural Office in Warsaw. In view of the principles of Polish foreign policy in the case of Taiwan, it was not possible to conclude an act regulating the avoidance of double taxation in the form of an international agreement. The legal status of Taiwan has led to the signing of an agreement between two representatives with competence, including the promotion of economic, commercial and cultural relations between Taiwan and Poland. The proposed bill introducing double taxation regulations in Poland's relations with Taiwan removes the obstacle to the development of trade and economic relations. The Senate in its amendment proposes that this period be extended and closed in the buckle of 1917 and 1991. The first date is the Bolshevik Revolution, the second - 1991 is the dissolution of the Soviet Union. Of course, there are reasons to support this amendment. After 1959, repression also occurred until 1991, on a much smaller scale and, you can say, milder, but also these repressions took place. The Senate to the Sejm of St. the law introduced an amendment in Article. 2, in which the legislator provided for the possibility to benefit from the State budget up to 65% of the agricultural crop insurance contributions from one or more selected types of risks despite exceeding the tariff rates of the insurance set out in the Act. The provision of this Article sets out in detail the percentage of aid and the manner in which it is reduced in proportion to the percentage of aid increases in excess of the rates, but does not specify exactly the intention of the legislator. For this reason, the Senate amendment is absolutely necessary as it clarifies this regulation. This provision is transitional as it will apply only in 2017. Its introduction was motivated by the fear that insurance companies would not want to insure farmers in the preferred package insurance. Until now, farmers have been insured against one or more risks. The result of this action is that at present only 10% agricultural holdings are insured for about 30% of their crops surface. This is usually the case for commodity holdings producing on the market and the insurance applies to crops that are most exposed to weather risks. The problem was also limited offer of insurance companies for agriculture and the level of insurance rates too expensive for many farmers. Government-planned Prime Minister Beata Szydło in the draft next year's budget, an increase of almost five times in funds for agricultural and livestock insurance has aroused interest in both farmers and insurance companies. Currently, only three insurance companies offered insurance to farmers in the field of agricultural crops. The aim of the Act is to promote crop and livestock insurance and to increase competitiveness among insurers. This can also be done by the plan of the Ministry of Agriculture to set up an Association of Agricultural Mutual Insurances. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-15-sejm-32-3.u305	Thank you very much, sir. And please, Mrs Ewa Lieder, the Modern Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-15-sejm-32-3.u306	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! The Senate adopted a proposal with one clarification amendment. This amendment clarifies the rules in Article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 on the tariff rates of insurance with a view to reducing the ambiguities of interpretation. In addition, the Senate specified the rule concerning the reduction in the amount of aid for agricultural insurance premiums in 2017. The amendment proposed by the Senate does not change the meaning of the Act, but it is worth noting that funding from the crop budget with such a high risk, which makes it necessary to set a contribution rate of 15% and more, it raises serious doubts as to whether it is not throwing money into the mud. Shouldn't these measures be aimed at combating losses and not at financing loss compensation after they occur? Droughts are a major threat to crops in recent years, but compensation for losses will not improve the situation, but will only increase problems. It would be much more effective to use these resources to improve the water balance of Poland, to fight against the phenomenon of a decrease in the content of caries in the soil, but also to work on the structure of such crops, which would be more resistant to adverse conditions. Now that we have such a huge budget deficit, it is important that spending is rational and that it gives hope that it will improve the situation in the future. Simple handing out to alleviate financial problems will only shift the financial disaster. When debating the issue of agricultural insurance, it must be borne in mind that a small number of insurance companies involved in this type of insurance result, among other things. the difficulties in the construction of the product, the lack of experience in the elimination of the damage and the imposition of the pricing method (fixing the maximum price levels at which the farmer is entitled to the contribution), the lack of profitability in the short term. As has already been said in the discussions on this project, amendments to the agricultural insurance law are made every year. In the longer term, steps should be taken to improve the insurance system. In many countries, e.g. in Austria, the problem of insurance in the case of drought or profitability in plant and animal production is solved through an index-based insurance system. In Italy, Hungary or Spain, income stabilisation instruments are put in place to improve the efficiency of dealing with instability and uncertainty of income on agricultural holdings irrespective of their origin (e.g. production or market). The payments from the State budget to the insurance premium will be up to 65% when the agricultural producer enters into a contract containing a set of risks, where the insurance undertaking determines a tariff rate not exceeding 9% of the insurance sum. Insurance against any risks, even those that may not concern the farmer concerned, may not be that large [[♪ The bell ♪]] enthusiasm for this insurance system. It is modern with farmers, but with a help that will solve problems in agriculture and not shift them over time. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
