Speaking of problems on the part of the Union, we are talking about two cases of the EU Court of Justice: against Germany and against Ireland. In both judgments, the EU Court of Justice stressed that operators established by national rules whose funding is made from compulsory contributions imposed by national producers' law do not have the same freedom to promote products as voluntary producer organisations. According to the judgment, the action of these entities must be attributed to a Member State and must respect the rules on the free movement of goods within the European Union. Therefore, they should not create measures similar to quantitative restrictions or discriminate against products originating in other Member States for the promotion of domestic products. Minister Babalski mentioned in his recent discussion on the project on the implementation of the This is, of course, a very necessary and important programme, but it has nothing to do with promotion funds, which are unlimited and, above all, involve much more money for the promotion of Polish products both in Europe and beyond. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-15-sejm-32-3.u384	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! [[Applause]] [[There is a Greater Poland Uprising, of course.]] The Committee on Culture and Media adopted this proposal unanimously after first reading and considering the draft at its meeting on 15 December 2016 and asks unanimously that the High Sejm adopt this resolution. [[Is there a Smolensk appeal in its contents?]] And here's what it says: [[The Gathered Rise Up]] The resolution of the Polish Sejm on the anniversary of the outbreak of the Greater Poland Uprising. The Wielkopolska Uprising 1918-1919 is an exceptional event against the backdrop of the Polish national uprisings. Its success brought freedom to the Wielkopolskas and contributed to the consolidation of independence and the establishment of the western border of the Republic of Poland reborn after 123 years of captivity. The uprising was preceded by many months of political procedures and organizational and military preparations of Polish society in the Prussian partition. The arrival of Ignacy Jan Paderewski to Poznań on 26 December 1918 met with enthusiastic reception of Polish residents. The next day the fights broke out, which in a short time led to the liberation of almost all of Poznań. The popular, popular break was based on the excellent organization of the Polish society, the efficiency of civil and military authorities, joint and several cooperation of all social groups. At the head of the Polish movement was the District of the General Council of the People's Republic of Poland, which included, among others, Fr. Stanisław Adamski and Wojciech Korfanty. Insurgent troops were commanded by Major Stanisław Taczak and then General Józef Dowbor-Muśnicki, organizer of the Wielkopolska Army. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland on the anniversary of the outbreak of the Wielkopolska Uprising reminds of the political maturity and dedication of Poles. The sacrificial struggle for the Independent is worthy of commemoration. Glory be to the Winners! [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-15-sejm-32-3.u500	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Yesterday, the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry carried out the first reading of the parliamentary draft law amending the Nature Conservation Act and some other laws contained in print No 1143, and then examined it. The committee's report is contained in print No 1148. The draft law presented by the group of Members responds to the demands of many landowners who have for years expressed their dissatisfaction and discomfort caused by the rules on the cutting of trees and shrubs resulting in a serious reduction in the rights of property owners. The project presented by the Group of Members of the Law and Justice, which was discussed yesterday by the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, leads to simplification of the regulation on the cutting of trees and shrubs, and to equip local authorities with the competence to adapt the level of protection of greenery to their needs and to differing national conditions, which are characterised by different localities. Let me remind you, High Izbo, that the current regulations require the owner of the plot to apply to the right uncle, mayor or city president for permission to cut out trees or shrubs, which is undoubtedly some kind of interference to the right of the owner of the property to dispose of the property. Let us add that the overwhelming majority of these decisions taken by municipalities in this respect are positive. So why the administrative nuisance? The project in question aims to enable municipalities to refrain from obtaining permits for the removal of trees and shrubs in situations which the local authorities consider justified and to waive fees for the removal of trees and shrubs if the local authority so decides. The municipalities will have the power to set, in relation to the maximum rate set by the Law, the rates of removal fees for trees and shrubs. Those maximum rates referred to in Article. The 85th Nature Conservation Act is 500 PLN for the removal of trees and 200 PLN for the removal of shrubs. In addition, the changes introduced reduce the scope of administrative control of clippings, e.g. by increasing the value of tree circuits for which there is no obligation to obtain authorisation and reducing the financial burden on applicants by increasing the value of tree circuits for which there is no obligation to pay for their removal. There will also be a change in the principle of measuring the circumference of trees. Now it will be driven at an altitude of 130 cm from the ground, not, which was often impossible, 5 cm from the ground. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The competent council of the municipality will be able to determine, by means of a resolution forming an act of local law, that the rules on the fees for the cut-off also do not apply to other trees or shrubs identified on the basis of criteria which take account of the species, age, circumference of the tree, the purpose of its - the tree or shrub - of the removal, the characteristics of the property on which they are located, its use in the local spatial planning plan or the types of land on which the trees or shrubs grow, or other criteria. Thus, as in the field of spatial policy, the municipality council will have in its hand a tool to conduct nature conservation policy in the area of the local municipality. The facilities described above for property owners are included in the article. 1 draft law. High Izbo! Art. 2 in turn contains amendments to the Forest Act. The proposed amendments aim at a closer link between the Forest Law and the environmental protection system, which regulates the protection of individual elements of the Forest Law. It is therefore proposed that the Minister for the Environment should lay down, by means of a regulation, the requirements of good forest management practice, taking into account the need to preserve all resources, creations and nature components in good condition, in particular those species which are of interest to the European Union, but also taking into account social, economic and cultural circumstances and local characteristics. High Izbo! Art. 3 introduces a transitional provision resulting from the systemic amendment of Environmental Law, i.e. the Act of 9 October 2015 amending the Act on the provision and protection of environmental information, public participation in environmental protection and environmental impact assessments, which enters into force on 1 January 2017. One of the changes is the separation of environmental impact assessments of forest management plans and the assessment of forest management's compliance with nature conservation requirements. Article 4 is replaced by the following: 3 is a transitional provision because it introduces a transitional period of one year, i.e. shall postpone entry into force until 31 December 2017. Let me remind you that the current wording of the article. 3 in the draft, the report of the committee shall read as follows: Until 31 December 2017, the forest economy shall be deemed not to be in breach of the prohibitions referred to in Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation. In short, Art. The 12 Birds Directives contain implementation and Article. 16 of this Directive contains exceptions to its implementation. It is similar with the second of the EU directives - in Article. 5 are provisions which implement it and in Article. 9 in turn there are derogations from the Birds Directive. The opinion submitted by the Sejm Analysis Office raises doubts as to the fact that Article. 3 the project does not refer to the specific conditions for the admissibility of derogations from the prohibitions set out in Article. 16 Habitats Directives and Article. 9 Birds Directives and these conditions will not be taken into account, for example, in situations where species and habitats are to be protected, which in the area covered by the forest management plan subject to a strategic environmental impact assessment only appeared after the plan was drawn up, i.e. that the proposed regulation does not take into account all the situations covered by the regulations of those Directives. For this reason, the wording of the article. 3 raises certain doubts as to the compatibility with the said Articles of the Directives. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-15-sejm-32-3.u502	Thank you. Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice to present a position on printing 1143, i.e. a draft law amending the Nature Conservation Act and some other laws. We consider the proposals put forward by the applicants to be reasonable. The proposed changes have been expected by society for many years, restore real property rights, reduce costs, eliminate unnecessary bureaucracy, i.e. procedure for unnecessary administrative authorisation to remove trees or shrubs growing on a private plot. We agree with the applicants that the proposed law aims to make the most urgent changes and we support these changes. I would like to remind you that the current legal situation does not allow for a reduction in the amount of the penalty for the removal of a tree without permission or a waiver of that penalty, e.g. because of the particular circumstances of the case, such as the health status of the cut-off tree, the nature and extent of the damage caused, the degree of culpability of the perpetrator or his motivation. There have also been numerous complaints to my parliamentary office about these provisions, as they did not provide for the possibility of taking account of justified circumstances. The lack of proportionality of the administrative penalty to the seriousness of the damage to the good has also been repeatedly raised. Ladies and gentlemen, on 1 July 2014, the Constitutional Tribunal examined complaints by private owners on which a public administration imposed penalties for the removal of trees without permission. And so on. for removing one poplar it was over 102 thousand. zł, one beech - almost 160,000. zł. The amount of the penalties was clearly not appropriate to the seriousness of the damage to the good, with which the Constitutional Tribunal agreed. This draft law aims to make changes that will allow a significant increase in the rights of property owners. The current restrictive state of law does not fulfil its role, as almost all the decisions made by public administrations are positive and thus allow the removal of trees. It is worth mentioning data: in 2014 and 2015 e.g. The municipality of Krościenko nad Dunajec issued 156 decisions, all positive. Lomza - 263 decisions, including two were negative. Gdansk e.g. Made nearly 1800 decisions, including only 15 were negative. The current procedure is therefore unnecessarily burdened on both property owners and public administrations. In addition, proposals for amendments to allow municipalities to refrain from obtaining permits for the removal of trees and shrubs in the situations they choose and to waive removal fees, including in the situations they choose. Such solutions will enable municipalities to adapt the scope of their proceedings to the real opportunities and needs of the municipality concerned. The aim of the proposed law is also to enable the conservation of all elements of the environment in an appropriate conservation state, in particular species of interest to the community and protected species of birds, in the forest area and in their vicinity. We also consider these solutions to be positive. It is worth noting in conclusion that the project has a positive socio-economic impact, has no impact on the state budget or local government units and is compatible with European Union law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

This law abolishes the obligation for a farmer to pay fees. Little is going on - in general the need to request an administrative decision on the removal of trees is abolished in this case, so this process greatly improves. Mrs Borowiec gave examples which clearly show that almost 98% decisions are positive decisions. Why is this happening? Because over the years of the current rules, there have been dozens of judgments of courts which have repealed the decision prohibiting the removal of trees, explaining this by restricting property rights. The officials knew - they had access to these judgments and learned - that they had to make a positive decision, because it is necessary, because if they do not, they violate property rights. It is practically possible to say that the State Forests are already in line with this code, it is only necessary to collect this code, so to speak, in whole, because the State Forests are engaged in sustainable forest management on the basis of a number of their own internal rules, which simply need to be collected and systematically arranged into appropriate blocks, so that they correspond to the scope of the laws, Birds and Habitats Directives. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-15-sejm-32-3.u542	Dear Marshal! High Seym! In a few days, December 19th, it is the 110th anniversary of the birth of Major. Stefan Rychter - Officer of the Polish Army and the Home Army, Chief of Staff of the Wachlarz. Stefan Rychter was born in 1906 in Nowa Słupi, a village between Ostrowiec Świętokrzyski and Kielce, at the foot of the Holy Cross. He was the son of Bolesław and Marianna of Kozłowski. In 1919, he began his studies at the State Men's Secondary School in Ostrowiec Świętokrzyskie, a school that still exists and has been named Joachim Chreptowicz since 1923. Her students were among others. B. Tadeusz Dulny - martyr from KL Dachau, Major Jan Piwnik The latter was Mrs Jadwiga Kaczyńska's uncle. It's important to mention these few names to show the atmosphere Stefan Rychter was studying at this school. And it was, in short, an atmosphere filled with patriotism. After a sentence in 1925 he started his law studies at the University of Warsaw. However, he was not given the opportunity to become a lawyer, because after finishing in July 1928 with the second place of the School of the Artillery Reserves in Włodzimierz Wołyński decided to serve in the Polish Army. Between 1928 and 1931 he studied at the School of Podchorażych Engineering in Warsaw, which he will finish with a high fifth place. On August 15, 1930, he was promoted to lieutenant and in 1935 to lieutenant. Since 1937, he has been a student of the Higher War School. In September 1939 he fought in ranks 3. The Division of the Legions Infantry, and after its break-up from 9 September he held the duties of chief of staff of the group of troops of this division led by colonel. Stanislaw Tatar. The documents preserved show that he was doing very well with the tasks entrusted to him. He was taken into captivity on 19 September, escaped from a POW camp in Tarnów and entered his home town. As early as October 1939 he joined the conspiracy activity, and since November this year as a representative of the chief Polish Victory Service General Michał Karszewicz-Tokarzewski was responsible for the merger of groups and conspiracy organizations in Kielce. Since 1940 he has been an officer of the Kielce District Command of the Armed Fighting Union, and since August 1941 he has been head of the Union of Retribution at the District Command of Kraków ZWZ. In the spring of 1942, Rychter was transferred to Warsaw, where he became Chief of Staff of the Wachlarz from April. As chief of staff of this organization, he demonstrated the qualities of a good organizer in particularly difficult conditions in which his soldiers came to work. On 19 January 1943, he moved to Minsk with the task of organising the release from the local prison of a dozen soldiers arrested in December 1942, among whom he was among others. Commander of section IV of the "Wachlarza" section, Major Tadeusz Sokołowski "Trop" section. It was a suicide mission. The strange thing was that the command of such a difficult and dangerous operation was entrusted to a high-ranking officer, not to one of the silent ones. Rychter left for Minsk full of anxiety, felt that this expedition could end in disaster. And in spite of these feelings, he follows the order, goes to Minsk, and there, betrayed by the Minsk prison guard, dies in the fight against the Germans, but his life is occupied by the death of five of them. The significant thing was that Major Tumry died a few days before his wedding. His choice was a soldier of the Home Army, a liaison, [[♪ The bell ♪]] who had also been arrested by the Germans a few days earlier. It wasn't until after the war that she found out about her bride's death. Recalling this Polish fate today, it resembles the figure of Major. I would like to stress that he remained faithful to his soldier's oath until the end. Hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-15-sejm-32-3.u546	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! It is my honor to present a statement about the railroads and the railroads in Chojnice. It's been 145 years since the first train entered the Chojnice station in November. As part of the royal railway of the eastern Prussian aggressor through Chojnice led the railway line from Berlin to Gdansk and Królewiec. After the construction of the line to Człuchów in 1877. The chords have become a railroad hub. In the following years of partitions further development of the node and station in Chojnice took place, there were lines to Nakła and Kościerzyna. Through the Chojnice there was Nord Express from Paris to the east of Europe, to Riga and St. Petersburg, then the capital of Russia. After Poland regained independence, Chojnice became a border town and the station in Chojnice was a border crossing. It is the Chojnice railway station, the railway workers accepted the first hit of the Germans during World War II. On 1 September 1939, at 3 p.m. 4.23 to the station in Chojnice instead of the announced train relations Berlin - Chojnice - Tczew - The Royale entered the armored train from which the station was attacked. Among the first victims and prisoners of World War II were the pioneers of the railway. During the years of the Polish People's Republic, the railway node in Chojnice belonged to the District Directorate of the State Railways in Gdańsk. The locomotive, together with the staff of the auxiliary units - the carriage, the traffic and trade units, the station service - was for many years the largest workplace in the Chojnic district, employing more than 2.5 thousand people. persons. Hard work, especially traction teams first serving steam locomotives, then combustion, today, after years, deserves respect and admiration. 18-year-old boys working on steam trucks during the 12-hour service moved 10 tons of coal to the boiler pit. Several days' service, sleeping in accommodation facilities throughout Poland during the running of freight warehouses - this was the everyday life of train drivers and helpers and conductor teams. In the 1970s, the Chojnicki node received about 100 pairs of trains a day, freight warehouses transited from GDR through Dziadowowo and Braniewo to the Soviet Union. Among the trains run by Chojnice were also military warehouses with nuclear warheads. In the period between August and September 1980, the company committees of the NSZZ.Solidarity were established at the junction of the PKP in Chojnice. The first president was Zbigniew Reszkowski, an engineer and his deputy were Maria Chrapkiewicz, Janusz Rolbiecki and Jan Buczek. The railwaymen from Chojnice actively joined in the activity of Solidarity, acting in the pastoral ministry of the people of work. Many members of the union organization were detained after the introduction of the martial law and were persecuted by their employer. Zbigniew Reszkowski was interned in Potulice under absurd allegations of organizing armed action in a militarized workplace. After regaining independence in 1989, thanks to the very good contacts of Chojnice railwaymen with decision-makers, the importance of the railway junction was significantly increased. It was renamed the rolling stock plant, which employed more than 4.5 thousand. employees. The Department of Regional Transports was established in Chojnice, which employed about 2 thousand people. employees. New rolling stock plant facilities were created, which were put into service on the eve of the railroad holidays on 23 November 1991. The solemn sacrifice was made by a sleeper. Bishop Marian Przykutki. Unfortunately, after the transformation of the system and shock therapy funded by Leszek Balcerowicz to Poles, rail and rail transport in Poland have lost significant importance. This phenomenon was not missed by the Chojnice Railwayrs either. Today, after many years of railway history in Chojnice, railroaders sadly say they have no successors. Young people do not have the opportunity to learn the professions connected with the operation and running of trains, railway schools shared the fate of many vocational schools in Poland. Rail work is difficult and responsible and therefore unattractive for young people entering the labour market. Many families survived from the work of their fathers on the railroad, many of the Chojnic families are related to the railroad. Worshiping and remembering tragic events [[♪ The bell ♪]] I pay tribute to the railwaymen, the victims of the treasonous German attack on Chojnice on 1 September 1939, their hard work in the post-war years, their activity in the NSZZ Solidarity and involvement in the development of the railway junction in Chojnice. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-15-sejm-32-3.u560	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On December 31st, we will celebrate the 160th anniversary of the birth of Wojciech Kossak, one of the most famous Polish painters, a representative of painting on historical and battle subjects, co-author of the Battle of Racławice. Wojciech Kossak was born on the last day of 1856 in Paris. His father, Juliusz Kossak, painter and sketch artist, gave him his first lesson in drawing. Later he studied at the School of Drawing and Painting under Władysław Łuszkiewicz and then at the Munich Academy of Fine Arts. He also served as a volunteer for a year's military service completed with an officer's exam. The artistic success of the young painter began in the 1980s. In the 19th century, when his paintings bought the Imperial Museum in Pest, the Prague Museum, and even Emperor Francis Joseph himself to one of his castles. Kossak lived and created in Berlin and traveled to France and the United States on numerous occasions. He was the president of the Society of Friends of Fine Arts, he founded a painting group called "Zero." He was awarded among others. The Commander's Cross with the Star of the Order of the Rebirth of Poland and the Golden Wawrzyn Academic of the Polish Academy of Literature. He was an artist extremely talented and hardworking, associated with painting all his life. He painted events from the Napoleonic Wars and the November Uprising, numerous genre scenes and portraits. In his paintings he showed an interest in battle and history. He created such painting works as Olszynka Grochowska, Battle for the City, the Kościuszki Staff, the Artilery in Fire, the Battle of Raszyn and many others. One of his most famous works is created together with Jan Styka's painting of Panorama Racławicka. It is worth noting that Wojciech Kossak was a true patriot. He even decided to break contact with the Berlin court after the anti-Polish speech of Emperor Wilhelm II, even though the court had been a protectorate over his painting for several years. He also refused to create paintings for the Germans during World War II. Wojciech Kossak was the father of famous figures of Polish culture: Maria Pawlikowska-Jasnorzewska, Magdalena Samozwiec and Jerzy Kossak. The artist has permanently entered the canon of the most outstanding Polish painters and personalities of the world of culture. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-15-sejm-32-3.u561	The uniform doesn't make a good cop, but the belief that the most important value is the other man, and no matter what the circumstances, you should always stand up for his life. The long-standing governments of the PO-PSL coalition have contributed to the liquidation of many local police stations in municipalities. The reform of the previous authority, known as the reorganisation, from 2007 resulted in a reduction in the number of constables from 811 to 399. After the change of power in Poland, as a result of detailed analysis and assessment of the level of safety, it must be noted that this reform has significantly reduced the level of safety in individual municipalities and small towns. The lack of district police stations increases social unrest and lack of security. The successful restoration of district police stations by the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration has resulted in the reactivation of units also in the Kujawsko-Pomorskie voivodship. With undiscovered joy and great satisfaction, I would like to inform you that in recent days there has been an opening of the restored police stations in the district of Włocławska: Lubranec, Lubień Kujawski and Chodcz. The ceremony was celebrated by Deputy Minister Jarosław Zieliński, who not only made a solemn opening, but also rewarded well-deserved uniform and self-government staff by giving promotions, awards and medals. In his speech, he expressed his deep faith in the fact that the reactivated police offices will best serve the safety of the inhabitants, and the service of duty officers, based on the motto: among people and for people, will be an idea that they always have to respect. The reconstruction of police stations is a response to the expectations of residents and the efforts of local authorities. As Minister Jarosław Zieliński said, the program of restoring individuals has enabled the support of local authorities with the government. The efforts of local authorities, district authorities and regional politicians have carried out many years of efforts to improve the safety and quality of life of residents. It is proud of the fact that mutual cooperation policy brings such measurable and necessary results as a response to social expectations.
